## Blocked

- PR 7 / #159 was blocked at the six-pass limit. On 2026-10-24 the user explicitly authorized reopening for fixes and up to three additional review passes after PR 9a; this is now scheduled, not externally blocked. Original evidence: six-pass escalation limit reached with four unresolved P2s: hidden-message recovery loops; HTTP-only epoch replacement cannot recover; successful run-only refresh clears a linked source's failed-metadata marker; Session settings has no retry after transient read errors. Static code evidence: Conversation.tsx clears only returned missing IDs and queues the rest, without clearing on remove; TranscriptHandoff.install rejects every different epoch while HTTP clock handling only arms deadlines; agentSources.ts clears failedSources for runs-only successes; SessionActivityControls.tsx still calls non-retrying api while gating off its poll. These are not accepted tradeoffs. Review 6 also lacks regression coverage because that reviewer hit its runtime budget. Authorized follow-up: fix all four issues and obtain complete coverage within passes 7–9. If that new allowance is exhausted with a blocker, escalate again; it is not permission to merge with missing coverage. Branch/PR retain all code and green test evidence. PR 8a depends on merged PR 4, so proceed independently.

## Decisions made during implementation

- 2026-10-23, PR 1: split into compilation/staging (1a), shared helpers (1b), and standalone processes (1c). The 27 source modules total approximately 168 KB before conversion: deletion plus addition alone exceeds the 300 KB review diff limit, and the initial bundle adds 77 KB of diff. Each split keeps all shipping paths usable. The original bundle is the first branch commit, `02d3a37f30fdd03f5533c1f5a8fc67b44870ecec`, based on `0c772683f3483ab073a28b476709c355e29713da`.
- 2026-10-23, PR 1a: emit standalone `.mjs` files for both package support assets and Docker runtime images. Source imports may use `.ts`; staging rewrites those paths. Existing Docker entrypoint names and deployment/Bake targets remain valid.
- 2026-10-23, PR 1a: include two minimal baseline test repairs required for the merge gate, independently reproduced on untouched `origin/main` (`0c77268`). The Feed fixture inserted a native decision while its run was `running`, although production only presents pending decisions on `awaiting_approval` runs; seed the legal state and additionally assert that resuming hides the old decision. The 6,000-message transcript fixture exceeded Vitest's five-second timeout (8.6 seconds on this VM); give that one test a bounded 30-second budget without reducing its data, query-plan, or pagination assertions. No production behavior changes.

- 2026-10-23, PR 1b: the 13 declared helpers are now TypeScript, with their `.d.mts` files removed; source callers use `.ts`, and the root strict typecheck includes `runtime`. The remaining standalone modules stay `.mjs` until 1c. Runtime type-only imports are erased from the compiled graph.
- 2026-10-23, PR 1b compiler mismatches: (1) Claude steering, idle deadlines and steering receipts lacked typed deferred resolvers/timers/maps; annotated their existing lifecycle. (2) Diagnostic inputs were promised `unknown` but implementation accessed arbitrary error fields; local optional-field views preserve existing guards, including code/status filtering; the label filter accepts unknown to retain its redundant false comparison. (3) RuntimeFailure's assigned details property is now declared. (4) Redaction's blanket `any` result is replaced by string/unknown overloads with typed recursive traversal; the existing server redaction wrapper retains its independent public contract. (5) Native approval replies now expose `unknown` results, and an absent method retains its existing fall-through behavior. (6) Model effort records and provider transcript records are open JSON boundaries, while returned capabilities/traces/activities have checked public types; dynamic maps use string keys and existing guards remain intact. (7) File MIME lookup needs a string index; caught filesystem errors are viewed as Node errno records, preserving the original missing-file handling. (8) Upload offsets are optional for check-only requests; the existing Number.isSafeInteger guard rejects omitted transfer offsets, so the numeric view is used only with that guard unchanged. (9) Harness launch/initialization and protocol subprocess/RPC fields, callbacks, pending replies and notification queues had no declarations; now checked in source, with open provider JSON only at the wire boundary. (10) resultChunks accepts unknown as previously promised, validates the envelope unchanged, and treats a tool name as a string only after whitelist membership. (11) Previously undeclared exports (lines, Rpc, authorizationUrl, codexSkills, latestPrompt, slashCommand, claudeModels, claudeCatalog and receiveSteering) are now checked; failed steering receipts may carry an unknown id because malformed JSON input is echoed by the existing implementation. No declared server call contract required a behavioral repair; all 17 original server import sites were retargeted.

- 2026-10-23, PR 1c: convert the remaining 14 implementations; retain only the documented `host.mjs` compatibility launcher and no `.d.mts` under `runtime`; add type-only serialized input/session contracts. Source subprocess fixtures use `.ts`; existing supervised host commands retain `.mjs`. The broker chooses a TypeScript sibling only when it itself runs from source; package/container entrypoints stay `.mjs`. Existing common compilation from 1a handles all outputs. The shared-Codex isolation/approval/cancellation regression now runs against both source and compiled broker graphs.
- 2026-10-23, PR 1c types: serialized input excludes server-only signal/inbox; maintenance signals and native exit statuses are optional/nullable. Declare lifecycle fields, typed callbacks/collections and structural child streams preserve emitted behavior. JSON remains open only at existing boundaries; unknown error/receipt views retain guards. Storage cleanup uses discriminated candidates and checked outcomes. Dynamic host import preserves environment setup order. All 13 mismatch categories and their resolutions are enumerated in #146; no runtime guard removed.

- 2026-10-23, PR 2: one bus on the process SQLite connection; emit only wakes a macrotask, and data_version samples precede reads. Drain metadata (id/owner/run/type), leaving payloads behind socket byte limits. Owner subscriptions are notifications, not historical replay; sockets keep independent cursors and drain all replay pages. One shared five-second revision check covers eventless Cards/Done changes. Worker publications coalesce one claim attempt, running events fill further slots, release wakes again, empty claims wait for the two-second fallback. Keep all maintenance and session rechecks. `LOOP_CHANGE_POLL_MS` configures the foreign hint interval.

- 2026-10-23, PR 3: retain the four-argument emit call shape with a discriminated type/payload tuple derived from the strict event union; validate before INSERT and preserve v2 durable payload bytes. Existing run events carry run scopes because they are not complete projection rows. Revision-only hints explicitly name the nine legacy collections and reuse the cursor; clients must act on equal-cursor hints. V3 substitutes scoped invalidations for wire oversize and pages bounded batches; invalid historical/unknown future events invalidate all. Per-source reset capabilities stay partial/no deadlines/snapshot, all timers remain. Collection counters/listeners avoid refreshing unrelated pagers and the thread. Added complete producer-contract cases, v3 compiled server/companion replay and a real paired Docker proxy path.

- PR 8b pass-2 interim assessment: correctness completed with P2 retained execution ownership requiring explicit recovery after an unclean bare-host reboot, plus P3 cumulative backoff and locally refused capacity reported unknown. Security completed without confirmed findings; regression/lead remain pending. A changed boot/init identity proves a different namespace lifetime, not that every process outside this host's view stopped; workspace data may also have moved from a still-running host. Accepting explicit recovery preserves the takeover invariant. For an actual completed reboot, inspect `loop recover-execution`, confirm the previous host/container and its children stopped, run `loop recover-execution --stopped-generation UUID_FROM_OUTPUT`, then `loop start`. Cumulative backoff remains bounded at 5s and prevents repeated restart churn; saturation's unknown outcome is conservative and causes no replay, although the message is misleading for a request never sent. Latest consolidation: no P0/P1/security, one P2 and two P3s. Accept P2 exhausted GET recovery as explicit bounded-read policy: after three 5xx attempts, Later/memory/workflow displays can remain stale until another signal, manual refresh/reopen or online/pageshow/visibility wake. This stops indefinite background reads to persistently failing endpoints; it affects display freshness, not durable writes or server command authority. api-backoff.test.ts explicitly verifies exhaustion/no further reads; Later exposes Try again; workspace-events.ts wake calls changed(). Endpoint recovery alone is not promised. P3 legacy polls may accumulate cooldown reads; failed Social completion emit can reject a stored refresh and skip remaining readers. These limitations are recorded, not claimed fixed. Initial HTTP-cursor race remains unverified for nonempty narrow replay; empty replay emits broad invalidation. Other static limits: run-wide rewrite cost/overflow, direct final metadata, native reclassification/bookmarks, owner-change callback lifetime, deletion retention, omitted secrets/nested triggers/live paths.
